SHRI MAHARAJA SANAJAOBA LEISHEMBA (Manipur): Thank you Madam for giving 
me the opportunity to participate in the discussion on “Motion of Thanks on the Hon. 
President’s Address to both the Houses on 28 th January, 2026. ”  On behalf of the 
North-East people, I would like to express my heartfelt gratitude and thanks to our hon. 
President of India and hon. Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Modi for their great concerns 
and inclusiveness of North-Eastern Region with the rest of the country.  In her Address, 
hon. President stated in her speech that “2026” would be the foundational year for Viksit 
Bharat by 2047.  The nation celebrates with great honour (i) 150 years of Vande 
Mataram; (ii) 350th Martyrdom Day of Guru Tegh Bahadur; (iii) 150th Birth Anniversaries 
of Bhagwan Birsa Munda and Sardar Patel.    These milestones remind us of our rich 
heritage and sacrifices of our ancestors and inspire further momentum to our journey 
towards Viksit Bharat by 2047. 
 As I belong to North-East Region, where the dalits, the backward (pichhda), 
the deprived (vanchit) and the tribal communities settle dominantly, I would like to give 
more focus on North-East.  Our Government’s commitment is “Sabka Sath, Sabka 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
149

 
 
Vikas”:  (i) A decisive battle against major diseases like ‘Sickle Cell Anemia’ has been 
fought to save our tribal people.  Over 6.5 crore tribal citizens have already been 
screened; (ii) More than 400 Eklavya Model Residential Schools have been opened in 
tribal areas to avail quality education and better future for our tribal children; (iii) In the 
last 11 years, post-metric scholarships of more than Rs.42,000 crore have been given 
to Scheduled Tribe students and about 5 crore students have benefited from this 
initiative.    
 Under the leadership of hon. Prime Minister, the North-East Region is 
empowering as the “Growth Engine of India. ”  Due to the negligent approach of the 
then UPA Government towards the Region, the North-East was pushed towards 
instability and underdevelopment.  But with the vision of “Purvodaya”, our Government 
is ensuring ‘Inclusive Growth for the Region’, transforming it as ‘Growth Engine of India’ 
and bringing long-term stability in the Region.   
Under the Modiji’s Government, North-East has received unprecedented focus.  
One, under the PM Gram Sadak Yojana, 50,000 rural roads have been constructed for 
easy access to markets, schools and offices etc..  Second, for Manipur also, a total 
special assistance of Rs.3,356 crores from 2020 till January, 2026 (under Special 
Assistance to States for Capital Expenditure) is provided to the State in the form of 50 
years’ interest-free loans; interest costs are borne by Central Government entirely.                                                         
Number three, investment of Rs. 80,000 crores in Railways has brought broad-
gauge connectivity to Arunachal Pradesh, Tripura and Mizoram. Manipur was also 
connected with broad-gauge Railways network at Jiribam for passenger train in May, 
2016, and first goods train reached Rani Gaidinliu Railway Station, Tamenglong, 
Manipur in 2022. If the pending works, that is, from Rani Gaidinliu Station to Imphal is 
completed at the earliest, then Manipur will be included in Railways Map of India. By 
the way, Imphal Railway Station is also being constructed under Amrit Bharat Station 
Scheme.  
Number four is, a large health infrastructure and development project being 
prepared in the region, like (a) construction of a medical college in Sikkim, (b) a 
women and child hospital in Agartala, (c) a medical college worth Rs. 46 crore at 
Churachandpur District, Manipur, has already been inaugurated by hon. Home 
150
[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
Minister, Shri Amit Shahji, and (d) Assam will soon become a lifeline for semiconductor 
chips.   Thus, Purvodaya is fast transforming the North-East into a resilient growth 
engine of Viksit Bharat. Recently, on 13 th September 2025, our hon. Prime Minister 
dedicated the following major projects during his visit to Manipur. (1) New Civil 
Secretariat at Imphal-East Manipur for enhancing governance infrastructure. (2) IT-
SEZ building and new Police Headquarters at Imphal-East Manipur for expanding 
technology and security infrastructure. (3) Manipur Bhawans in Delhi and Kolkata for 
providing accommodation support for citizens from the State. (4) Ima Markets in four 
districts, establishing dedicated economic space for women traders of Manipur . (5) 
Imphal Airport, i. e., Bir Tikendrajit International Airport has been covered under Krishi 
Udan Scheme to assist farmers in transporting agricultural products, and (6) For 
developing tourism, over Rs. 117 crore has been sanctioned under Swadesh Darshan 
Scheme in Manipur. 
I once again express my heartfelt gratitude to the hon. President and the hon. 
Prime Minister for transforming the North-East region, including Manipur, into a resilient 
growth engine of India, to become Viksit Bharat by 2047.  With these few words, I 
conclude my speech on the Motion of Thanks on the President's address. Thank you, 
Madam.

Hon. Vice-Chairperson, I am reminded that the hon. President, in her 
President’s Address, cautioned us about the misuse of Artificial Intelligence (AI) and 
deepfakes, which is fast becoming a grave threat to democracy, social harmony, and 
public trust. The recent fake narratives around Operation Sindoor and the Special 
Intensive Revision (SIR) process demonstrate how misinformation can inflame 
emotions and distort the Opposition's stance, rather than encouraging informed 
debate. 
I congratulate the Government for courageously bringing sensitive issues like 
Operation Sindoor and SIR to Parliament for detailed discussion and for urging all 
parties to rise  above politics to protect national security, electoral integrity and dignity 
of democracy.

THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S . PHANGNON KONYAK) : Shri Ramji ; not 
present. Shri Naresh Bansal; not present. Shri P. P. Suneer. 
SHRI P. P. SUNEER (Kerala): Thank you, Madam, for giving me this opportunity to 
speak on the Motion of Thanks to the President's Address on behalf of my Party, the 
Communist Party of India. Disagreement is the soul of democracy. It is with that spirit 
that I rise to strongly differ several assertions made in the President’s Address, which, 
in my view, fail to reflect the lived realities of our people. The Sarkaria Commission 
reminded us that federalism is a basic feature of our Constitution. Yet, today, States 
like Kerala are forced to approach Courts even for disaster relief. Borrowing ceilings, 
delayed dues and arbitrary fiscal rules amount to a financial blockade. The Centre 
appears to be adopting a coercive approach towards opposition-ruled States, forcing 
neo-liberal policies and even withholding education funds to enforce compliance. The 
States like Kerala are facing extraordinary fiscal stress and they require immediate and 
substantial Central intervention and support. Federalism is not a favour from the Centre; 
it is a constitutional mandate.  
On labour reforms, the Government has dismissed over 29 Central labour laws, 
replacing them with four Labour Codes without meaningful consultation with workers. 
This is not reform; it is rollback. These changes weaken job security, dilute social 
protection and tilt the balance decisively against labour in favour of capital. Jobless 
growth, rising unemployment, skyrocketing price rise, widening inequality, widespread 
contractualisatiion of labour, insecurity and denial of the fundamental right to live are all 
what the last decade of the BJP regime has brought to the people.                                                            
Sir, the Economic Survey 2025-26 has suggested a “re-examination” of the 
Right to Information Act, 2005, enacted by the UPA-1 Government along with the 
MGNREGA, which marked a historic advance in institutionalizing transparency and 
democratic accountability.  The RTI framework has already been systematically 
weakened over the past decade by this Government.  Amendments enacted in 2019 
undermined the independence of the Information Commissions by placing the tenure 
and service conditions of the Information Commissioners at the discretion of the Central 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
161

 
 
Government.  Procedural changes to the RTI rules have made information harder to 
access.  Any further dilution of the RTI Act would be disastrous for democratic 
accountability and public trust. 
 On foreign    policy, lofty claims   of balance and vision ring hollow when India 
remains largely silent on major humanitarian crises- from Ukraine, Gaza to Venezuela.  
India's   approach is in a state of complete paralysis*  
Lately, the announcement and promotion of the so-called ‘Board of Peace’ as 
a peace initiative by Donald Trump, in reality, undermines the central role of the United 
Nations - the only universally-recognized body entrusted with upholding international 
law and sovereign equality of nations. The record of the Trump administration towards 
the United Nations exposes the real intent behind the ‘Board of Peace’.  During his 
tenure, the U.S. has withdrawn from 66 international organisations, out of which 31 are 
linked to the United Nations involved in activities such as climate change, international 
solar alliance, WHO, UN Human Rights Council, etc.  
THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S . PHANGNON KONYAK) :  Hon . Member, 
kindly conclude.  
SHRI P. P. SUNEER:  Now, I conclude. The Indian Government should condemn such 
actions of the United States and we should reject the invitation to join the ‘Board of 
Peace’.  Strategic ambiguity cannot substitute moral leadership. Thank you, Madam.  
THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S . PHANGNON KONYAK) :  Now, Shri 
Mokariya Rambhai.   
SHRI MOKARIYA RAMBHAI (Gujarat):   Hon. Vice Chairperson Madam, I would first 
extend my sincere and heartfelt thanks to the hon. President of India, Smt. Droupadi 
Murmu, for her deeply inspiring and motivational address to both Houses of Parliament. 
Today, I rise with great enthusiasm to speak in strong favour of the Motion of Thanks. 
It fills me with profound honour to address this august House, where I wish to express 
my deepest and most sincere gratitude to the hon. Prime Minister Shri Narendra Modi 
 
* Not recorded. 
 English translation of the original speech delivered in Gujarati.  
162
[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
for his truly visionary leadership and the extraordinary, transformative achievements of 
the Modi Government.  Since taking the helm in 2014, under Shri Narendra Modi’s 
capable and unwavering  stewardship,  India  has  scripted  an   truly   remarkable  and 
inspiring journey of all-round progress. His leadership has successfully lifted 25 to 27 
crore people out of the clutches of poverty, while building 4 crore sturdy pucca houses 
and delivering life-changing piped water connections to 12.5 crore households through 
the transformative Jal Jeevan Mission. The Direct Benefit Transfers, exceeding a 
staggering ₹6.75 lakh crore last year alone, serve as a powerful testament to 
unwavering transparency, perfectly embodying the noble principle of Sabka Saath, 
Sabka Vikas. At the same time, Ayushman Bharat has provided 11 crore free treatments 
to the poorest of the poor, bringing hope and healing to millions. 
Shri Narendra Modi’s dynamic Modi Government has completely revolutionized 
the agriculture sector, achieving a record-breaking harvest of 350 million tonnes of food 
grains, catapulting India to the pinnacle as the world's top rice producer and securing 
second position in both fish and milk production. Through the PM Kisan Nidhi scheme, 
a massive ₹4 lakh crore has been transferred directly and efficiently into the hands of 
our hardworking farmers. Infrastructure development has surged ahead in leaps and 
bounds: the PM Gram Sadak Yojana alone added 18,000 km of vital rural roads last 
year, contributing to a grand total of 50,000 km over the last 11 years. Our railways are 
on the cusp of 100% electrification (98 percent already completed), bolstered by over 
150 sleek Vande Bharat trains and breathtaking engineering icons like the Chenab and 
Pamban bridges. Metro networks have triumphantly crossed 1,000 km, propelling India 
to the third spot globally—a source of immense national pride that swells every heart. 
The economic reforms spearheaded by Shri Narendra Modi Ji shine with brilliant 
clarity and impact. GST has delivered savings of ₹1 lakh crore, while income tax 
exemptions up to ₹12 lakh offer much-needed boosts to the aspiring middle class. The 
new labour codes simplify and enhance welfare delivery, and the forward-looking 
SHANTI Act aims for an ambitious 100 GW of nuclear energy capacity. PLI schemes 
have ignited ₹17 lakh crore in production, with semiconductors and defence exports 
soaring to ₹23,000 crore. Mudra has channeled a colossal ₹38 lakh crore to empower 
youth entrepreneurship, giving rise to 2 lakh innovative startups. 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
163

 
 
Women-led development is thriving vibrantly under this vision: creating 2 crore 
empowered Lakhpati Didis, pioneering Namo Drone Didis, and welcoming the NDA's 
first trailblazing women cadets. Security achievements stand tall, from the decisive 
Operation Sindoor to shrinking Maoist presence in 8 districts. In the realm of space, 
proud milestones like Shubhanshu Shukla's historic ISS mission signal the exciting 
arrival of Gaganyaan. 
In our country, an astounding 81 crore people are receiving 5 kilograms of free 
foodgrains every month, provided at the generous annual cost of Rs. 2.53 lakh crore 
rupees—a true lifeline for millions. An hon. Member from the opposition just observed 
that the ideal requirement of foodgrains per person stands at 12 kilograms, yet only 5 
kilograms per person are being distributed by the Government. I wish to humbly submit 
that these 5 kilograms are offered purely as a vital helping hand; after all, our people 
engage in various occupations and livelihoods as well, and this thoughtful benefit 
meaningfully supports and strengthens them.   
We all remember too well the dark shadow of the COVID-19 pandemic that 
gripped the world in the past. During that crisis, the citizens of our nation were 
vaccinated entirely free of cost, ensuring their protection from this formidable threat. 
Moreover, hon. Modiji went beyond borders to distribute the COVID vaccine to more 
than 100 nations around the world. It is for this reason that Modiji is held in deep respect 
across the globe. He truly embodies an icon of humanity in the midst of the pandemic. 
Modiji has saved millions of precious lives not only in India but across the world. Leaders 
from various countries of the world revere Modiji precisely because he saved the lives 
of so many people by generously supplying vaccines to numerous nations. While 
leaders of the world generally adhere strictly to protocol, their profound affection and 
respect for hon. Modiji compel them to go that extra heartfelt step, greeting him with 
genuine warmth and affection. 
Gujarat—Shri Narendra Modi Ji's cherished home State and my own beloved 
home state too—beautifully exemplifies these resounding national successes. As Chief 
Minister, he pioneered the iconic Vibrant Gujarat Summits, attracting billions in 
transformative investments. Remarkably, a summit in Saurashtra concluded just 10 
days ago, drawing investments worth crores of rupees—indeed, a matter of great and 
164
[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
justifiable pride. The current Chief Minister of Gujarat, Shri Bhupendrabhai Patel’s 
dedicated efforts have played an important and pivotal role in the resounding success 
of this Summit, and I thank him profoundly for this. Today, Gujarat leads boldly in solar 
energy with the expansive Charanka Park, establishes GIFT City as a premier global 
financial hub, and witnesses Mundra Port managing massive cargo volumes with 
efficiency. The State proudly tops the ease of doing business rankings, features world-
class highways like the Delhi-Mumbai Expressway extension, and shines through dairy 
cooperatives like the legendary Amul. Gujarat's ambitious semiconductor push and 
green hydrogen projects align perfectly with the vision of Viksit Bharat, generating lakhs 
of jobs and showcasing how the Modi Government's Gujarat model has been scaled 
masterfully across the nation. 
The innovative PRAGATI scheme has accelerated a whopping ₹85 lakh crore in 
key projects, while the Purvodaya scheme is uplifting the Northeast with 7,200 km of 
crucial highways. Previous Governments did not pay sufficient attention to 
infrastructure projects in North East India, but Modiji has focused with determination 
on enhancing railway connectivity, robust road infrastructure, and the Gram Sadak 
Yojana in the North Eastern states, resulting in holistic development across the region. 
The PM JANMAN scheme commits ₹1 lakh crore for the welfare of tribals—echoing 
Gujarat's own successful tribal development via the Dharti Aaba schemes.

India), SAMRUDDHA BHARAT (Prosperous India), AND AATMA-NIRBHAR BHARAT 
(Self-dependent India). This would be the true and fitting way of thanking the Hon'ble 
President of India for her inspiring Address to the Parliament. 
Hon. Members, Shri Narendra Modi Ji's unifying leadership brings us all together 
in pursuit of Viksit Bharat by 2047. On behalf of Gujarat and this esteemed House, I 
extend my most heartfelt thanks to our visionary Prime Minister.  With this, I 
wholeheartedly support this motion of thanks for the Address delivered by the Hon'ble

In conclusion, Madam, Vice-Chairperson, let me say that India, in 2026, is not 
a country hiding behind walls, but a country building bridges -- bridges between East 
and West, North and South, present and future. We are blending. All these moves are 
interconnected. They signal the arrival of India as a confident global player, one that 
speaks with its own voice. The President's Address has captured this ethos eloquently. 
And, today, I humbly expand on it with specifics to borrow a phrase -- India is better 
equipped than most to withstand the turbulence of a managed disorder of the world. 
Not just to withstand, but to lead and shape it. I urge our friends in the Opposition to 
also see the larger picture, the initiatives referred to by the hon. President are not just 
random or elite-centric. (Time-bell rings.) Just a minute, Madam! They are part of a 
168
[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
strategic blueprint for India @ 100, securing a rightful place in the world for the benefit

Naya Bharat rather than fulminating from the sidelines. As a parliamentary democracy, 
we can have spirited debates, but when India engages the world, let us do so in one 
voice and as one team -- Team India.

President’s Address. Let us carry this vision forward. India is on the rise and we will rise

THE VICE- CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S. PHANGNON KONYAK) : Now, hon. 
Member, Shri Manas Ranjan Mangaraj. 
SHRI MANAS RANJAN MANGARAJ (Odisha): Madam, I rise to speak on the Motion 
of Thanks on the hon. President's Address. We respect the hon. President, but this 
House must also raise the real concerns of the people. For Odisha, several serious 
issues need urgent attention. Madam, the first issue is the Mahanadi river dispute. Due 
to urgent upstream barrages in Chhattisgarh, the water flow to Odisha has reduced. 
This is severely affecting agriculture, drinking water and livelihood of farmers in many 
districts. The Mahanadi Water Dispute Tribunal's tenure is ending on 13th April, 2026. If 
its tenure is not extended, justice will be delayed. I strongly urge the Government to 
immediately extend the Tribunal's tenure, so that a fair and final decision can be 
ensured.  
The second major issue is the Polavaram project. This project poses a serious 
risk of submergence in parts of Malkan district of Odisha, particularly vulnerable tribal 
groups may be affected due to higher flood levels and backwaters. There are concerns 
regarding environmental clearances, consultations and rehabilitation. Development 
cannot come at the cost of the most vulnerable citizens. Therefore, strict compliance 
with environmental laws and proper rehabilitation norms is essential to protect tribal 
communities.  
Madam, Odisha is a resource-rich State, contributing coal and mineral to the 
nation. However, coal royalty rates have not been revised for over a decade. Mining-
affected regions continue to face poverty, pollution and displacement. A fair revision of 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
169

 
 
coal royalty is necessary, so that the State can invest in local development. Madam, 
Odisha has also been demanding special category status or equivalent financial 
support. 
The State faces recurrent cyclones, floods and climate-related disasters.  It also 
has large tribal and backward regions.  Extra support from the Centre is justified on 
grounds of both equity and need.   
(MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN in the Chair.) 
Sir, our farmers need fair MSP and timely procurement.  Paddy farmers in 
Odisha often suffer from delays and distress sales.   Rising input costs are increasing 
their burden.  Unemployment is another serious issue. Many young people from Odisha 
migrate outside the State due to lack of local opportunities.  We need stronger support 
for industries, skill-based employment and job creation. These are not only Odisha’s 
issues, they concern federal fairness and national welfare.  Therefore, I respectfully 
urge the Government to extend the Mahanadi Tribunal tenure immediately, ensure full 
legal and environmental compliance in the Polavaram Project to safeguard tribal 
communities, revise coal royalty and grant Special Category Status or equivalent 
support, ensure fair MSP, timely procurement and better employment opportunities.  
True development means justice for every region and protection for the most 
vulnerable. Jai Jagannath! 
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN :  Thank you, Manas Ranjan ji.  Now, Message from Lok 
Sabha, Secretary-General.

MOTION OF THANKS ON PRESIDENT'S ADDRESS- Contd. 
SHRI HARIS BEERAN (Kerala):  Sir, the President was forced to read a script by the 
Government which was far from the truth.  The President spoke about Amrit Kaal.  But 
for the widows in the Wayanad, who were the victims of Wayanad landslide tragedy, 
who continue to receive notices for repayment of loan from the banks, for the fishermen 
in Kerala’s coast watching corporate trawlers stealing their catch by juvenile fishing, for 
the research scholars belonging to the Backward Class communities who are denied 
scholarships, for the labourers of this country who have been denied the benefits of 
MGNREGA law, this is not Amrit Kaal.  This is Rahu Kaal - a darkness manufactured 
by policy.  The Government claims five trillion dollar economy but could not find Rs.18 
crore to waive off the loan of the Wayanad victims. You write off lakhs of crores of 
rupees loans to the corporate sector but, on the pretext of some technicalities, you do 
not give this benefit to the poor.  This is moral bankruptcy disguised as governance.   
Sir, we woke up in the morning today hearing about an Indo-US trade deal on 
the social media. The nation has been kept in dark about the happenings.  The 
Parliament is in session.  It was the duty of the Government to apprise the nation as to 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
171

 
 
what is happening.  It is a blot to our sovereignty when the President of a foreign country 
states that India will not buy oil from Russia and India will buy oil from Venezuela, which 
is the country of his choice.  Now, there is nobody to respond to this, whether it is 
actually a blot to our sovereignty or not. The hon. President spoke about the Viksit 
Bharat of 2047.  But when Sensex crashed 1,100 points on the day of the Budget, the 
hard-earned money of the middle-class investors was wiped away.  …(Interruptions)…  
It was wiped away.   
Now, let me speak about the minorities.  Hon. President failed to mention that 
the education funding for the minorities was shrinked from Rs.678 crores to Rs.55 
crores. There is a 92 per cent cut on the budget for the minority education. Now, Sir, 
as far as the Madrasa modernization is concerned, it is a huge boost by the Government 
by giving one lakh rupees for the Madrasa modernization, so much so for the children 
of the Minority communities.* 
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: No comments, please. ...(Interruptions)... 
SHRI HARIS BERRAN : Indian Union Muslim League, whom I am representing, is a 
historic party, where it was part of the Freedom struggle, and it was a part of the 
Constituent Assembly. This party was the chief architect of coalition politics in Kerala 
because of which the Minorities and the backward classes got representation in power. 
It is because of that education could be granted to the Minorities, and education could 
be granted to the backward class citizens. It is a matter of shame that not a single 
Muslim Minority is there in the present Cabinet. So much so, as when they talk about 
inclusiveness, not even a single Minority representation is there in the present day 
Cabinet.   
Now, Sir, you privatize the nuclear power sector, through the SHANTI Act and 
capped liability at Rs. 3,000 crores. You have socialized risk, privatized profit and 
removed supplier accountability. You have reduced citizens to test subjects for foreign 
technologies. 
 
* Not recorded.

[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
 Now, Sir, in conclusion, I say, we, the people of India, require a Viksit Bharat

concluding.  Development is not measured by the heights of the statues or corporate 
profits, but by dignity afforded to the most vulnerable.  On behalf of Kerala and India's 
silenced voices, I dissent.  Jai Hind!

MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN:  Dr. Sarfraz Ahmad, not present. Shri S. Selvaganabathy. 
SHRI S. SELVAGANABATHY (Puducherry):  Hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I am happy 
to extend my heartfelt gratitude to the hon. President for the inspiring Address to both 
the Houses of Parliament.  Her words reflect the aspirations of a billion citizens and the 
vision of a nation marching confidently towards a brighter future.  In supporting the 
Motion of Thanks, I wish to highlight some of the important points that define our 
collective journey.  Ensuring dignity for gareeb through inclusive growth, under the 
leadership of hon. Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Modi, the Government has focused 
on freeing the gareeb from the vicious cycle of poverty and enabling them to progress 
with dignity.  As a result of targeted and transparent welfare schemes, nearly 25 crore 
Indians have risen out of poverty.  Reflecting this commitment, over 4 crore pakka 
houses have been built for the poor in the last decade, including 32 lakhs in the last 
year.  Through the Jal Jeevan Mission, piped drinking water has reached 12.5 crore 
new families in five years, with one crore added in the last year alone.  More than 10 
crore households have also received LPG connection under the Ujjwala Yojana.  By 
eliminating leakages through DBT, the Government has ensured direct, corruption-free 
delivery of benefit worth Rs.6.75 lakh crore, transforming welfare into empowerment 
and laying the foundation for a more inclusive and aspirational India.                                       
The Modi Government aims at holistic welfare of our annadatas, that is, our farmers, 
and anchors Viksit Bharat on annadata’s prosperity, positioning agriculture as the 
institutional foundation of national development. A testament to this commitment is the 
transfer of rupees four lakh crore directly to farmers’ accounts, establishing measurable 
income security under PM Kisan Accounts. 
The Modi Government honours the honest taxpayer.  For decades, the middle-
class, 31 per cent of India's population, slaved as the silent engine of growth. They 
worked the hardest, yet the UPA Government mercilessly trapped even low-income 
families in the tax net with a pitiful exemption of rupees two lakh. Families were blood-
dry, dreams were deferred, and aspirations were crushed under heavy burdens. 
176
[RAJYA SABHA]

 
 
Today, the current policy has made annual income up to rupees twelve lakh completely 
tax-free. This is a prosperity premium introduced by the Modi Government that puts 
back thousands of rupees into the pockets of over 3.3 crore salaried citizens every 
month. 
While earlier taxpayers were burdened by a rigid two-year window for 
corrections, the timeline for updating income tax returns has now been doubled to four 
years. Similarly, the TDS threshold on rent has been increased from Rs. 2.4 lakh to Rs. 
6 lakh. This is not mere relief; it is justice for the honest taxpayer, ending the UPA's era 
of exploitation and fuelling the aspirations of every young professional, home buyer, 
and parents. 
Now, I come to education for all.  From scarcity to holistic excellence, the middle 
class is no longer forced to settle for a stagnant education system defined by the 
scarcity mindset of the Congress and the rote learning methods of the pre-2014 era. 
For over six decades, before 2014, successive Congress Governments presided over 
chronic neglect, adding just a handful of seven AIIMS in 60 years and barely expanding 
medical colleges to mere 387, leaving millions of aspiring doctors and middle-class 
families in despair. 
In contrast, under Prime Minister Narendra Modi's bold vision, we have 
shattered those barriers, and the number of AIIMS has been increased to 23, which is 
more than tripple; medical colleges have nearly been doubled to 808. Undergraduate 
medical students have surged by 141 per cent and postgraduate students by 144 per 
cent. Universities have jumped from 723 to 1168 in the last decade.  From launching the 
PM Vidyalakshmi scheme for student financial aid to providing one crore internship 
opportunities in top companies, the focus has shifted from just granting degrees to 
creating a future-ready workforce for Viksit Bharat. 
My next point is about engineering sovereignty and global benchmark.  The Modi 
Government's guarantee to a Viksit Bharat by 2047 is visible in the rapid delivery of 
engineering marvels across our diverse landscape, from the Chenab Bridge, the 
world's highest railway bridge in Jammu and Kashmir, to the Atal Tunnel in Himachal 
Pradesh and the Atal Setu in Mumbai. In stark contrast, the Congress-led UPA era was 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
177

 
 
defined by deliberate neglect, chronic delays, policy paralysis, and stalled projects 
worth rupees 18 lakh crores from 2011 to 2014. 
However, the most remarkable of these is the new Pamban Bridge in Tamil 
Nadu, India’s first vertical lift sea bridge and only the second of its kind globally.  Built 
at a cost of approximately Rs.550/- crore, this 2.08 kilometer structure features a 
ground-breaking 72.5 meter navigational span that rises vertically 17 meters in just five 
minutes.   This Rs.550/- crore investment replaces a century-old British-era structure 
with a 21 st Century marvel that can withstand the harshest marine corrosion, 
symbolizing a nation that no longer just dreams but delivers world-class infrastructure. 
 As regards restoring cultural pride and spiritual connectivity, guided by the 
philosophy of ‘ Vikas Bhi, Virasat Bhi’, the new Pamban Bridge is more than just 
concrete and steel; it is a restoration of a  sacred link to Rameswaram,  the holy land, 
from where, according to the Ramayana, the construction of Ram Setu was initiated.  
Inaugurated by the hon. Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Modi, on the auspicious day of 
Ram Navami, this bridge connects the mainland to one of India’s Char Dham pilgrimage 
sites, fulfilling a demand that persisted for decades.  In stark contrast, the Congress 
ignored this vital spiritual and infrastructural need.  Despite persistent local demands, 
visible damage from the 2013 naval barge incident, and repeated breakdowns of the 
old 1914 bridge, they delivered only patchwork repairs and endless delays, stranding 
pilgrims and residents in inefficiency for years together.  Unlike the old 1914 bridge 
where trains were restricted to crawling 10 kmph, the new electrified structure supports 
heavy rail traffic and faster trains at 75 kmph, ensuring that our spiritual heritage is 
supported by modern efficiency. 
 Coming to Vande Mataram 150: a lyrical prayer for Viksit Bharat, as India enters 
the second phase of this Century, the nation commemorates the 150 th anniversary of 
the composition of Vande Mataram. (Time-bell rings) 
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN:  Please conclude.  Time is over. 
SHRI S. SELVAGANABATHY: Unlike Congress leaders who refused to sing the song 
during public events, the Modi Government now celebrates this ‘lyrical prayer’ through 
a year-long nationwide festival.  Thank you, Sir.

[RAJYA SABHA]

Concern over the rising incidences of crimes and the erosion of public safety in 
Society 
DR. SASMIT PATRA (Odisha):  Madam, I draw the attention of the Government to a 
deeply troubling national trend which is the steady rise in violent crimes, gender-based 
offences and targeted attacks on vulnerable communities, leading to a serious erosion 
of public safety and constitutional assurance.  While policing is primarily a State subject, 
the nature, scale and persistence of such crimes, and their direct bearing on 
fundamental rights, internal security, and the implementation of Central laws and 
schemes, make this an issue that warrants the attention of the Central Government. 
Over the past two years, multiple cases of heinous crimes against women and 
girl children, including sexual violence, have shaken public conscience and exposed 
serious gaps in prevention, child protection and timely justice. There have also been 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
189

repeated reports of violence, intimidation and social coercion against Scheduled 
Tribes, Scheduled Castes and minorities raising concerns about the effective 
enforcement of constitutional safeguards and special protection laws. 
Instances of gang violence, murders and communal tensions point not merely 
to isolated failures, but to systemic weaknesses in early intervention, investigation and 
accountability. Such conditions undermine public confidence in institutions and 
weaken social harmony, which is essential for development and national integration. 
I, therefore, urge the Union Government to (a) strengthen the implementation 
of laws relating to crimes against women and children, including fast track courts and 
victim support and (b) ensure effective enforcement of protections for Scheduled 
Tribes, Scheduled Castes and minorities.       
THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S. PHANGNON KONYAK):  The hon. Member, 
Shri Madan Rathore (Rajasthan), associated himself with the Special Mention made 
by the hon. Member, Dr. Sasmit Patra. 
Demand for Establishment of a branch of Central Board of Film 
 Certification (CBFC) in Gujarat 
SHRI KESRIDEVSINH JHALA (Gujarat):  Madam, I bring to draw the attention of the 
Government to the remarkable rise and contributions of the Gujarati Film Industry to 
Indian cinema. Historically, Gujarati entrepreneurs, producers, directors and actors 
have played an essential role in shaping the cinematic landscape of our country.       
Thanks to the progressive policies of the Gujarat Government and ongoing 
technological advancements, the Gujarati film sector has witnessed rapid growth. From 
releasing just 10–20 films annually, the industry now produces over 75 films each year, 
with annual investments surpassing Rs.150 crore. Further, around 125–150 Censor 
Certificates are issued annually for a diverse range of content, including feature films, 
advertisements, short films, documentaries and web content. However, at present, 
Gujarati producers and Preview Committee members are compelled to travel to 
Mumbai and other locations for CBFC screenings. This results in significant logistical 
and financial strain. The establishment of a CBFC branch in Gujarat, ideally in 
190
[RAJYA SABHA]

Ahmedabad or Gandhinagar, would greatly benefit all stakeholders by ensuring timely 
processing, reducing travel costs and easing the burden on the Mumbai office. 
Moreover, such a branch could extend its services to contents in Kutchhi, Sindhi and 
potentially Rajasthani-Marwadi languages, thereby enhancing cultural representation 
from the region. Under the visionary leadership of hon. Prime Minister, Gujarat has set 
benchmarks in several sectors, and it is now poised to lead in cinematic infrastructure 
as well.  
I, therefore, urge the Government to kindly expedite the process of establishing 
a branch of CBFC at Ahmedabad or Gandhinagar at the earliest. Thank you.   
THE VICE- CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S. PHANGNON KONYAK): The following hon. 
Members associated themselves with the Special Mention made by the hon. Member, 
Shri Kesridevsinh Jhala: Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha), Shri Madan Rathore (Rajasthan) 
and Shri R. Girirajan (Tamil Nadu). 
Now, Shri Sanjay Singh; not present. Then, Shri Tiruchi Siva. 
Demand for implementation of a National Road Safety Mission 
SHRI TIRUCHI SIVA (Tamil Nadu) : Madam, road safety is a multifaceted challenge, 
spanning policing, public health, social behaviour, and urban planning and mobility. 
Adequate attention is required to be paid to these sectors. The Supreme Court has 
flagged that electronic enforcement standards need to be implemented, but little action 
has been taken in this regard. Ambulance availability and emergency preparedness vary 
widely, especially in rural areas. The lack of timely trauma care often leads to 
preventable deaths. Separate initiatives focusing on road safety currently exist, 
undertaken by Central and local authorities. However, such patchwork legislation and 
fragmented strategy have yielded weak results. The number of road accidents and their 
severity has been steadily increasing. In 2024 alone, 1.77 lakh people have died in road 
accidents, which average to about 485 persons a day. More than 4.6 lakh people have 
been injured in 2023. Over-speeding is responsible for 7 in 10 of these deaths.   
India’s road accident fatality rate stands at 11.89 per 1, 00, 000 people. This 
poses a great economic burden of roughly three per cent of the country’s GDP annually. 
[ 3 February, 2026 ]
191

Against this backdrop, human-centric interventions– which prioritize pedestrian 
safety, public transportation and strengthen trauma care facilities – are essential. 
Coordinated efforts across agencies, alongside strict enforcement of traffic laws, could 
save many lives. I urge the Government to establish and implement a National Road 
Safety Mission. Thank you.       
THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI S. PHANGNON KONYAK): The following hon. 
Members associated themselves with the Special Mention made by the hon. Member, 
Shri Tiruchi Siva: Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha), Shri Haris Beeran (Kerala) and Shri R. 
Girirajan (Tamil Nadu).
